href="https://x.com/VibeMarketer_">@VibeMarketer_</a></div></div><div class="flex shrink-0 items-center gap-1 whitespace-nowrap"><button data-slot="xds-menu-trigger" type="button" tabindex="0" aria-haspopup="menu" aria-expanded="false" id="base-ui-_R_1lkojlcpip8j6_" aria-label="More" class="group relative inline-flex cursor-pointer items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-full font-semibold select-none transition-colors focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-ring disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:opacity-70 aria-disabled:cursor-not-allowed aria-disabled:opacity-70 h-8 min-w-8 text-subtext1 [&amp;_svg:not([class*=&#x27;size-&#x27;])]:size-4 text-primary hover:bg-btn-ghost-hover aria-expanded:bg-btn-ghost-hover active:bg-btn-ghost-pressed w-8 px-0 [&amp;_svg]:transition-transform [&amp;_svg]:duration-200 group-hover:[&amp;_svg]:scale-110 -my-2 shrink-0" style="border-radius:9999px"><span class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-0 flex items-center justify-center transition-opacity opacity-0"><svg aria-hidden="true" class="animate-spin size-4 text-[color:inherit]" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><circle class="opacity-25" cx="12" cy="12" r="10" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="4"></circle><path class="opacity-75" d="M4 12a8 8 0 018-8V0C5.373 0 0 5.373 0 12h4z" fill="currentColor"></path></svg></span><span class="inline-flex items-center whitespace-nowrap" style="opacity:1"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" fill="currentColor" data-icon="icon-more" viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="1em" height="1em" display="flex" role="img" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M3 12c0-1.1.9-2 2-2s2 .9 2 2-.9 2-2 2-2-.9-2-2zm9 2c1.1 0 2-.9 2-2s-.9-2-2-2-2 .9-2 2 .9 2 2 2zm7 0c1.1 0 2-.9 2-2s-.9-2-2-2-2 .9-2 2 .9 2 2 2z"></path></svg></span></button></div></div></div></div><div dir="auto" class="font-chirp max-w-full whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-text text-body font-normal"></div><!--$--><div class="flex flex-col gap-3"><img alt="Article cover image" class="aspect-5/2 w-full object-cover" decoding="async" loading="eager" src="https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HQkkPg2b0AAQ8OQ?format=webp&amp;name=medium"/><h1 dir="auto" class="font-chirp max-w-full whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-text text-title2 font-extrabold">How to Build a Company Brain That Gets Smarter Every Week</h1><div class="x-article-body break-words"><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">I am going to show you how to turn every correction, decision, and useful workflow your team gives AI into one company brain that makes everyone better.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Because right now, the smartest AI in your company probably works for one person.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One teammate has a Claude chat that understands the strategy. Another has taught Codex how you ship. Someone else has shown ChatGPT exactly how the founder wants proposals written.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Multiply that across every employee and agent. Your company is creating an incredible amount of intelligence, then trapping it inside private chats and personal setups.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A company brain changes that.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">It turns what one person teaches AI into a better starting point for everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Here is the build:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">find the intelligence already hiding in your team&#x27;s AI work;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">give every agent the same map of the company;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">turn corrections into shared learning; and</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">build the first version in HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Throughout the article, I will give you working examples you can hand straight to your agent: the folder structure, map file, correction loop, and full build prompt.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Copy the blocks, paste them into your agent, and build along as you read.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. Your company already has a brain. It is scattered everywhere.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The sales lead explains which objection kills the deal. The marketer corrects an old product claim. The operator shows an agent which numbers can be trusted. The founder rewrites the same sentence for the fifth time.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That is your company teaching AI how it thinks. Almost none of it escapes the chat.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">So everyone gets faster, but each person ends up working with a slightly different version of the business.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One agent thinks an old campaign is current. Another follows a preference that was never approved. A third produces brilliant work because one employee happens to know the magic prompt.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The company is learning everywhere and remembering almost nowhere.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. A company brain makes your best work reusable</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Think of a company brain as everything your team has already figured out, made available to the next person and the next agent.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">When someone begins a task, they should not have to reconstruct the company from scratch. They should be able to find:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">what the company believes now;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">which decisions replaced older decisions;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">who owns the final call;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">how this kind of work should be done;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">which claims, sources, and examples are approved; and</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">what the team learned the last time it did this.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A new marketer starts with the positioning the founder spent months sharpening. The sales agent gets the objection handling that already works. Ops gets the workflow the team has already debugged.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Nobody needs the original chat or even needs to know who figured it out. The lesson belongs to the company now.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The value of a company brain is simple: learn once, improve everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. The brain should know how your company thinks, not only what it knows</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Most company knowledge projects collect meeting notes, strategy decks, old campaigns, customer calls, product docs, and years of messages. Then they place a search box on top and call it a brain.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That gives AI more information. It does not give AI better judgment.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A useful company brain carries four things:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Memory: the facts, decisions, and history that matter now.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Judgment: the rules and standards that shape good work.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Capability: the skills and workers the company can run again.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Learning: the reviewed corrections that improve everything else.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Now connect them. One agent finds a trusted answer. Another inherits it. One worker solves a blocker. The next starts with the solution.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">At that point, the brain stops behaving like a library and starts coordinating the company.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The brain becomes valuable when it can remember, decide, act, and share what worked.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">4. Start with one workflow that matters</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Start with the workflow your team is tired of explaining.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Maybe every content brief needs the same ten corrections. Maybe customer handoffs fall apart unless one specific person is involved. Maybe the Monday report takes two hours because nobody trusts the same numbers.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Pick one. The team should run it often, know what good looks like, and be able to review the result quickly.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Now ask:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What would an excellent employee need to know, believe, and do to complete this well?</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">For a content brief, that might mean current positioning, approved claims, audience pain points, voice rules, strong examples, and the final format.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your first company brain can be this small:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">company-brief.md: what we do and who we serve</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">priorities.md: what matters right now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">decisions: what we decided and why</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">people: owners and approval boundaries</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">projects: current work and status</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">policies: rules that survive every chat</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">skills: proven ways to perform a task</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">workers: repeatable jobs an agent can run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">learnings.md: corrections worth keeping</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Paste that block into your agent and ask it to create the structure around your chosen workflow.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Leave anything you cannot maintain empty for now. One small brain that removes a painful weekly mistake is worth far more than a beautiful archive nobody uses.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">5. Give every agent a map, then let the work pull the details</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The first file every agent reads should be a tiny map of the company.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">It does not need the entire business. It needs enough to understand where it is, what matters, and where to look next.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Use this as the starting point:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Company map</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What we do</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe the company, customer, offer, and positioning in one short paragraph.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What matters now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. [Priority one]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. [Priority two]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. [Priority three]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Source order</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. [The system that owns current facts]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. [The system that owns project status]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. [The system that owns customer commitments]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Navigation</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- decisions contains what we decided and why</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- people contains owners and approval boundaries</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- projects contains current work and status</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- policies contains rules that apply every time</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- skills contains proven ways to perform tasks</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- workers contains repeatable jobs agents can run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- learnings.md contains reviewed corrections and lessons</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Rules</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- use the newest approved information</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- when two sources conflict, follow the source order</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- if the answer is missing, say what you could not find</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- never turn a guess into company knowledge</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">This file is the front door. Paste the block into your agent, fill in the blanks together, and let each task decide which room it needs to enter.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A content agent pulls positioning, approved claims, and voice. A customer agent pulls account history and escalation rules. A coding agent pulls architecture decisions and security policy.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">They all use the same brain without dragging the entire company into every conversation.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That matters because more context can create more confusion. Old campaigns compete with current positioning. Loose meeting notes compete with approved decisions. History starts looking like policy.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The fix is not more memory in every prompt. It is better navigation.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Give the agent the map first. Let it earn every deeper piece of context by needing it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">6. The correction is often more valuable than the output</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Say a content agent writes a strong proposal but uses last quarter&#x27;s positioning.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Most people fix the sentence, ship the proposal, and move on. The document gets better. Nothing else does.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The useful question is why the old positioning was still there.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Maybe the company brief is stale. Maybe nobody recorded the new decision. Maybe an old campaign is outranking the approved language.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Fix that layer, and the correction survives the proposal.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Here is where each fix should go:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Missing or outdated fact → company knowledge</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">New strategic choice → decision record</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Repeated preference → policy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Proven technique → skill</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Repeatable sequence → worker</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Dangerous action → mechanical gate</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Then review the change before sharing it with everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The loop looks like this:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">work → correction → route → review → share → better future work</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Run that loop for long enough and every project leaves the company slightly smarter than it found it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">7. The brain needs one main home</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Folders work brilliantly when one person owns the whole thing.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Then the second person joins. Someone edits a policy. Someone else uses the old version. A useful skill stays trapped on one laptop.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Add Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, and one company brain quickly splits back into five personal ones. It needs one main home.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">This is why we built HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">HQ keeps the company layer together while everyone keeps using the AI tool they prefer.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">When one teammate finds a better way to do something, the change moves through review and into Main HQ. Once it is accepted, the rest of the team gets it too.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One teammate discovers a better way.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The improvement is reviewed.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Main HQ learns.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Every teammate starts further ahead.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One person figures something out on Tuesday. By Wednesday, the whole team can be working from it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One person teaches the brain. The whole company remembers.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">8. Paste this into your agent and build the first version now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Pick one workflow that matters, replace the brackets, and paste this into an agent with access to your company workspace.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Do not spend a month designing the perfect brain. Use this to create one useful loop, run it on real work, and improve it from the corrections.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Help me build the first version of our company brain around one recurring workflow.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Company</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe the company, customer, offer, and current priorities.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Workflow</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Name one recurring workflow the team already performs.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Outcome</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe what a strong completed result looks like.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your job</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. Identify the minimum company knowledge required to complete this workflow well.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. List the decisions, policies, preferences, and approval boundaries that should guide the work.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. Identify the people, projects, and systems that own the current truth.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">4. Create a short company map that tells future agents where to find each type of context.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">5. Design one repeatable worker with clear inputs, steps, output, and stopping conditions.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">6. Create a correction-routing table for facts, decisions, policies, skills, workers, and safety gates.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">7. Define the human review required before any improvement becomes shared company knowledge.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Return</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the minimum company-brain structure</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the source hierarchy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the first policy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the first worker specification</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the correction and review loop</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the missing information you need from me</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the exact first test we should run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Keep the first version small. Do not ingest the entire company. Build only what this workflow needs, and explain why every file or layer exists.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Run the first test. Every hesitation, guess, or stale answer shows you what the brain needs next.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">You can build the first version in a folder. When you want the whole team to use it across Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, bring it into HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Start with one workflow in HQ. Add the context it needs, run it, fix what breaks, and share the improvement with everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your team is already teaching AI how the company works every day.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">HQ makes sure the company gets to remember it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">If you want to test HQ with your own team, go to <a class="text-[#1d9bf0] no-underline hover:underline" href="https://www.hqforwork.com/r/vibe-marketer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">https://www.hqforwork.com/r/vibe-marketer</a></p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Follow me at @VibeMarketer_ for more. Thanks for reading :)</p></div></div><!--/$--><div class="flex flex-col gap-1 empty:hidden"></div><div class="shrink flex items-center text-gray-700 [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:content-[&#x27;·&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:px-1 [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:shrink-0"><span class="contents"><a id="_R_5jlcpip8j6_" data-state="closed" class="font-chirp max-w-full whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-gray-700 text-body font-normal hover:underline inline-flex w-fit" href="/VibeMarketer_/status/2092243372929151135">1:31 PM · Aug 25, 2026</a><script nonce="Ps0+ToKaq2w4kVj0LZ9ObQ==">!function(options){var element=document.getElementById(options.targetId);if(!element)return;var cached=document.querySelector('[data-timezone]');var timeZone=cached&&cached.dataset.timezone||Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone;element.dataset.timezone=timeZone;element.textContent=(function formatFullTimestamp({ timestamp, lang = "en", timeZone }) {

to Build a Company Brain That Gets Smarter Every Week",preview_text:"I am going to show you how to turn every correction, decision, and useful workflow your team gives AI into one company brain that makes everyone better.\nBecause right now, the smartest AI in your",cover_media_results:$R[43]={__ref:"QXBpTWVkaWFSZXN1bHRzOgwAAQoAAR0JJD4Nm9AACgACAAAAAAAAAAAAAA=="},id:"QXJ0aWNsZUVudGl0eToyMDkyMjQzMzY2NzU5MjM1NTg2",rest_id:"2092243366759235586",plain_text:"I am going to show you how to turn every correction, decision, and useful workflow your team gives AI into one company brain that makes everyone better.\nBecause right now, the smartest AI in your company probably works for one person.\nOne teammate has a Claude chat that understands the strategy. Another has taught Codex how you ship. Someone else has shown ChatGPT exactly how the founder wants proposals written.\nMultiply that across every employee and agent. Your company is creating an incredible amount of intelligence, then trapping it inside private chats and personal setups.\nA company brain changes that.\nIt turns what one person teaches AI into a better starting point for everyone.\nHere is the build:\nfind the intelligence already hiding in your team's AI work;\ngive every agent the same map of the company;\nturn corrections into shared learning; and\nbuild the first version in HQ.\nThroughout the article, I will give you working examples you can hand straight to your agent: the folder structure, map file, correction loop, and full build prompt.\nCopy the blocks, paste them into your agent, and build along as you read.\n1. Your company already has a brain. It is scattered everywhere.\nThe sales lead explains which objection kills the deal. The marketer corrects an old product claim. The operator shows an agent which numbers can be trusted. The founder rewrites the same sentence for the fifth time.\nThat is your company teaching AI how it thinks. Almost none of it escapes the chat.\nSo everyone gets faster, but each person ends up working with a slightly different version of the business.\nOne agent thinks an old campaign is current. Another follows a preference that was never approved. A third produces brilliant work because one employee happens to know the magic prompt.\nThe company is learning everywhere and remembering almost nowhere.\n \n2. A company brain makes your best work reusable\nThink of a company brain as everything your team has already figured out, made available to the next person and the next agent.\nWhen someone begins a task, they should not have to reconstruct the company from scratch. They should be able to find:\nwhat the company believes now;\nwhich decisions replaced older decisions;\nwho owns the final call;\nhow this kind of work should be done;\nwhich claims, sources, and examples are approved; and\nwhat the team learned the last time it did this.\nA new marketer starts with the positioning the founder spent months sharpening. The sales agent gets the objection handling that already works. Ops gets the workflow the team has already debugged.\nNobody needs the original chat or even needs to know who figured it out. The lesson belongs to the company now.\nThe value of a company brain is simple: learn once, improve everyone.\n3. The brain should know how your company thinks, not only what it knows\nMost company knowledge projects collect meeting notes, strategy decks, old campaigns, customer calls, product docs, and years of messages. Then they place a search box on top and call it a brain.\nThat gives AI more information. It does not give AI better judgment.\nA useful company brain carries four things:\nMemory: the facts, decisions, and history that matter now.\nJudgment: the rules and standards that shape good work.\nCapability: the skills and workers the company can run again.\nLearning: the reviewed corrections that improve everything else.\nNow connect them. One agent finds a trusted answer. Another inherits it. One worker solves a blocker. The next starts with the solution.\nAt that point, the brain stops behaving like a library and starts coordinating the company.\nThe brain becomes valuable when it can remember, decide, act, and share what worked.\n \n4. Start with one workflow that matters\nStart with the workflow your team is tired of explaining.\nMaybe every content brief needs the same ten corrections. Maybe customer handoffs fall apart unless one specific person is involved. Maybe the Monday report takes two hours because nobody trusts the same numbers.\nPick one. The team should run it often, know what good looks like, and be able to review the result quickly.\nNow ask:\nWhat would an excellent employee need to know, believe, and do to complete this well?\nFor a content brief, that might mean current positioning, approved claims, audience pain points, voice rules, strong examples, and the final format.\nYour first company brain can be this small:\ncompany-brief.md: what we do and who we serve\npriorities.md: what matters right now\ndecisions: what we decided and why\npeople: owners and approval boundaries\nprojects: current work and status\npolicies: rules that survive every chat\nskills: proven ways to perform a task\nworkers: repeatable jobs an agent can run\nlearnings.md: corrections worth keeping\nPaste that block into your agent and ask it to create the structure around your chosen workflow.\nLeave anything you cannot maintain empty for now. One small brain that removes a painful weekly mistake is worth far more than a beautiful archive nobody uses.\n5. Give every agent a map, then let the work pull the details\nThe first file every agent reads should be a tiny map of the company.\nIt does not need the entire business. It needs enough to understand where it is, what matters, and where to look next.\nUse this as the starting point:\nCompany map\n\nWhat we do\n\n[Describe the company, customer, offer, and positioning in one short paragraph.]\n\nWhat matters now\n\n1. [Priority one]\n2. [Priority two]\n3. [Priority three]\n\nSource order\n\n1. [The system that owns current facts]\n2. [The system that owns project status]\n3. [The system that owns customer commitments]\n\nNavigation\n\n- decisions contains what we decided and why\n- people contains owners and approval boundaries\n- projects contains current work and status\n- policies contains rules that apply every time\n- skills contains proven ways to perform tasks\n- workers contains repeatable jobs agents can run\n- learnings.md contains reviewed corrections and lessons\n\nRules\n\n- use the newest approved information\n- when two sources conflict, follow the source order\n- if the answer is missing, say what you could not find\n- never turn a guess into company knowledge\nThis file is the front door. Paste the block into your agent, fill in the blanks together, and let each task decide which room it needs to enter.\nA content agent pulls positioning, approved claims, and voice. A customer agent pulls account history and escalation rules. A coding agent pulls architecture decisions and security policy.\nThey all use the same brain without dragging the entire company into every conversation.\nThat matters because more context can create more confusion. Old campaigns compete with current positioning. Loose meeting notes compete with approved decisions. History starts looking like policy.\nThe fix is not more memory in every prompt. It is better navigation.\nGive the agent the map first. Let it earn every deeper piece of context by needing it.\n \n6. The correction is often more valuable than the output\nSay a content agent writes a strong proposal but uses last quarter's positioning.\nMost people fix the sentence, ship the proposal, and move on. The document gets better. Nothing else does.\nThe useful question is why the old positioning was still there.\nMaybe the company brief is stale. Maybe nobody recorded the new decision. Maybe an old campaign is outranking the approved language.\nFix that layer, and the correction survives the proposal.\nHere is where each fix should go:\nMissing or outdated fact → company knowledge\nNew strategic choice → decision record\nRepeated preference → policy\nProven technique → skill\nRepeatable sequence → worker\nDangerous action → mechanical gate\nThen review the change before sharing it with everyone.\nThe loop looks like this:\nwork → correction → route → review → share → better future work\nRun that loop for long enough and every project leaves the company slightly smarter than it found it.\n7. The brain needs one main home\nFolders work brilliantly when one person owns the whole thing.\nThen the second person joins. Someone edits a policy. Someone else uses the old version. A useful skill stays trapped on one laptop.\nAdd Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, and one company brain quickly splits back into five personal ones. It needs one main home.\nThis is why we built HQ.\nHQ keeps the company layer together while everyone keeps using the AI tool they prefer.\nWhen one teammate finds a better way to do something, the change moves through review and into Main HQ. Once it is accepted, the rest of the team gets it too.\nOne teammate discovers a better way.\nThe improvement is reviewed.\nMain HQ learns.\nEvery teammate starts further ahead.\nOne person figures something out on Tuesday. By Wednesday, the whole team can be working from it.\nOne person teaches the brain. The whole company remembers.\n \n8. Paste this into your agent and build the first version now\nPick one workflow that matters, replace the brackets, and paste this into an agent with access to your company workspace.\nDo not spend a month designing the perfect brain. Use this to create one useful loop, run it on real work, and improve it from the corrections.\nHelp me build the first version of our company brain around one recurring workflow.\n\nCompany\n\n[Describe the company, customer, offer, and current priorities.]\n\nWorkflow\n\n[Name one recurring workflow the team already performs.]\n\nOutcome\n\n[Describe what a strong completed result looks like.]\n\nYour job\n\n1. Identify the minimum company knowledge required to complete this workflow well.\n2. List the decisions, policies, preferences, and approval boundaries that should guide the work.\n3. Identify the people, projects, and systems that own the current truth.\n4. Create a short company map that tells future agents where to find each type of context.\n5. Design one repeatable worker with clear inputs, steps, output, and stopping conditions.\n6. Create a correction-routing table for facts, decisions, policies, skills, workers, and safety gates.\n7. Define the human review required before any improvement becomes shared company knowledge.\n\nReturn\n\n- the minimum company-brain structure\n- the source hierarchy\n- the first policy\n- the first worker specification\n- the correction and review loop\n- the missing information you need from me\n- the exact first test we should run\n\nKeep the first version small. Do not ingest the entire company. Build only what this workflow needs, and explain why every file or layer exists.\nRun the first test. Every hesitation, guess, or stale answer shows you what the brain needs next.\nYou can build the first version in a folder. When you want the whole team to use it across Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, bring it into HQ.\nStart with one workflow in HQ. whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-text text-body font-normal"></div><!--$--><div class="flex flex-col gap-3"><img alt="Article cover image" class="aspect-5/2 w-full object-cover" decoding="async" loading="eager" src="https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HQkkPg2b0AAQ8OQ?format=webp&amp;name=medium"/><h1 dir="auto" class="font-chirp max-w-full whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-text text-title2 font-extrabold">How to Build a Company Brain That Gets Smarter Every Week</h1><div class="x-article-body break-words"><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">I am going to show you how to turn every correction, decision, and useful workflow your team gives AI into one company brain that makes everyone better.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Because right now, the smartest AI in your company probably works for one person.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One teammate has a Claude chat that understands the strategy. Another has taught Codex how you ship. Someone else has shown ChatGPT exactly how the founder wants proposals written.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Multiply that across every employee and agent. Your company is creating an incredible amount of intelligence, then trapping it inside private chats and personal setups.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A company brain changes that.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">It turns what one person teaches AI into a better starting point for everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Here is the build:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">find the intelligence already hiding in your team&#x27;s AI work;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">give every agent the same map of the company;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">turn corrections into shared learning; and</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">build the first version in HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Throughout the article, I will give you working examples you can hand straight to your agent: the folder structure, map file, correction loop, and full build prompt.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Copy the blocks, paste them into your agent, and build along as you read.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. Your company already has a brain. It is scattered everywhere.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The sales lead explains which objection kills the deal. The marketer corrects an old product claim. The operator shows an agent which numbers can be trusted. The founder rewrites the same sentence for the fifth time.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That is your company teaching AI how it thinks. Almost none of it escapes the chat.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">So everyone gets faster, but each person ends up working with a slightly different version of the business.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One agent thinks an old campaign is current. Another follows a preference that was never approved. A third produces brilliant work because one employee happens to know the magic prompt.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The company is learning everywhere and remembering almost nowhere.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. A company brain makes your best work reusable</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Think of a company brain as everything your team has already figured out, made available to the next person and the next agent.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">When someone begins a task, they should not have to reconstruct the company from scratch. They should be able to find:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">what the company believes now;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">which decisions replaced older decisions;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">who owns the final call;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">how this kind of work should be done;</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">which claims, sources, and examples are approved; and</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">what the team learned the last time it did this.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A new marketer starts with the positioning the founder spent months sharpening. The sales agent gets the objection handling that already works. Ops gets the workflow the team has already debugged.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Nobody needs the original chat or even needs to know who figured it out. The lesson belongs to the company now.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The value of a company brain is simple: learn once, improve everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. The brain should know how your company thinks, not only what it knows</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Most company knowledge projects collect meeting notes, strategy decks, old campaigns, customer calls, product docs, and years of messages. Then they place a search box on top and call it a brain.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That gives AI more information. It does not give AI better judgment.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A useful company brain carries four things:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Memory: the facts, decisions, and history that matter now.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Judgment: the rules and standards that shape good work.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Capability: the skills and workers the company can run again.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Learning: the reviewed corrections that improve everything else.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Now connect them. One agent finds a trusted answer. Another inherits it. One worker solves a blocker. The next starts with the solution.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">At that point, the brain stops behaving like a library and starts coordinating the company.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The brain becomes valuable when it can remember, decide, act, and share what worked.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">4. Start with one workflow that matters</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Start with the workflow your team is tired of explaining.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Maybe every content brief needs the same ten corrections. Maybe customer handoffs fall apart unless one specific person is involved. Maybe the Monday report takes two hours because nobody trusts the same numbers.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Pick one. The team should run it often, know what good looks like, and be able to review the result quickly.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Now ask:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What would an excellent employee need to know, believe, and do to complete this well?</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">For a content brief, that might mean current positioning, approved claims, audience pain points, voice rules, strong examples, and the final format.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your first company brain can be this small:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">company-brief.md: what we do and who we serve</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">priorities.md: what matters right now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">decisions: what we decided and why</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">people: owners and approval boundaries</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">projects: current work and status</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">policies: rules that survive every chat</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">skills: proven ways to perform a task</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">workers: repeatable jobs an agent can run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">learnings.md: corrections worth keeping</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Paste that block into your agent and ask it to create the structure around your chosen workflow.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Leave anything you cannot maintain empty for now. One small brain that removes a painful weekly mistake is worth far more than a beautiful archive nobody uses.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">5. Give every agent a map, then let the work pull the details</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The first file every agent reads should be a tiny map of the company.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">It does not need the entire business. It needs enough to understand where it is, what matters, and where to look next.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Use this as the starting point:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Company map</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What we do</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe the company, customer, offer, and positioning in one short paragraph.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">What matters now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. [Priority one]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. [Priority two]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. [Priority three]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Source order</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. [The system that owns current facts]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. [The system that owns project status]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. [The system that owns customer commitments]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Navigation</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- decisions contains what we decided and why</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- people contains owners and approval boundaries</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- projects contains current work and status</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- policies contains rules that apply every time</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- skills contains proven ways to perform tasks</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- workers contains repeatable jobs agents can run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- learnings.md contains reviewed corrections and lessons</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Rules</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- use the newest approved information</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- when two sources conflict, follow the source order</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- if the answer is missing, say what you could not find</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- never turn a guess into company knowledge</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">This file is the front door. Paste the block into your agent, fill in the blanks together, and let each task decide which room it needs to enter.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">A content agent pulls positioning, approved claims, and voice. A customer agent pulls account history and escalation rules. A coding agent pulls architecture decisions and security policy.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">They all use the same brain without dragging the entire company into every conversation.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">That matters because more context can create more confusion. Old campaigns compete with current positioning. Loose meeting notes compete with approved decisions. History starts looking like policy.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The fix is not more memory in every prompt. It is better navigation.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Give the agent the map first. Let it earn every deeper piece of context by needing it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">6. The correction is often more valuable than the output</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Say a content agent writes a strong proposal but uses last quarter&#x27;s positioning.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Most people fix the sentence, ship the proposal, and move on. The document gets better. Nothing else does.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The useful question is why the old positioning was still there.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Maybe the company brief is stale. Maybe nobody recorded the new decision. Maybe an old campaign is outranking the approved language.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Fix that layer, and the correction survives the proposal.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Here is where each fix should go:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Missing or outdated fact → company knowledge</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">New strategic choice → decision record</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Repeated preference → policy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Proven technique → skill</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Repeatable sequence → worker</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Dangerous action → mechanical gate</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Then review the change before sharing it with everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The loop looks like this:</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">work → correction → route → review → share → better future work</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Run that loop for long enough and every project leaves the company slightly smarter than it found it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">7. The brain needs one main home</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Folders work brilliantly when one person owns the whole thing.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Then the second person joins. Someone edits a policy. Someone else uses the old version. A useful skill stays trapped on one laptop.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Add Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, and one company brain quickly splits back into five personal ones. It needs one main home.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">This is why we built HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">HQ keeps the company layer together while everyone keeps using the AI tool they prefer.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">When one teammate finds a better way to do something, the change moves through review and into Main HQ. Once it is accepted, the rest of the team gets it too.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One teammate discovers a better way.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">The improvement is reviewed.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Main HQ learns.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Every teammate starts further ahead.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One person figures something out on Tuesday. By Wednesday, the whole team can be working from it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">One person teaches the brain. The whole company remembers.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">8. Paste this into your agent and build the first version now</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Pick one workflow that matters, replace the brackets, and paste this into an agent with access to your company workspace.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Do not spend a month designing the perfect brain. Use this to create one useful loop, run it on real work, and improve it from the corrections.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Help me build the first version of our company brain around one recurring workflow.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Company</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe the company, customer, offer, and current priorities.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Workflow</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Name one recurring workflow the team already performs.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Outcome</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">[Describe what a strong completed result looks like.]</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your job</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">1. Identify the minimum company knowledge required to complete this workflow well.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">2. List the decisions, policies, preferences, and approval boundaries that should guide the work.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">3. Identify the people, projects, and systems that own the current truth.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">4. Create a short company map that tells future agents where to find each type of context.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">5. Design one repeatable worker with clear inputs, steps, output, and stopping conditions.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">6. Create a correction-routing table for facts, decisions, policies, skills, workers, and safety gates.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">7. Define the human review required before any improvement becomes shared company knowledge.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Return</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the minimum company-brain structure</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the source hierarchy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the first policy</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the first worker specification</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the correction and review loop</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the missing information you need from me</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">- the exact first test we should run</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Keep the first version small. Do not ingest the entire company. Build only what this workflow needs, and explain why every file or layer exists.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Run the first test. Every hesitation, guess, or stale answer shows you what the brain needs next.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">You can build the first version in a folder. When you want the whole team to use it across Claude Code, Cursor, and Codex, bring it into HQ.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Start with one workflow in HQ. Add the context it needs, run it, fix what breaks, and share the improvement with everyone.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Your team is already teaching AI how the company works every day.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">HQ makes sure the company gets to remember it.</p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">If you want to test HQ with your own team, go to <a class="text-[#1d9bf0] no-underline hover:underline" href="https://www.hqforwork.com/r/vibe-marketer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">https://www.hqforwork.com/r/vibe-marketer</a></p><p class="mb-4 leading-relaxed whitespace-pre-wrap">Follow me at @VibeMarketer_ for more. Thanks for reading :)</p></div></div><!--/$--><div class="flex flex-col gap-1 empty:hidden"></div><div class="shrink flex items-center text-gray-700 [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:content-[&#x27;·&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:px-1 [&amp;&gt;span:not(:empty)~span:not(:empty)]:before:shrink-0"><span class="contents"><a id="_R_1cspcpip8j6_" data-state="closed" class="font-chirp max-w-full whitespace-pre-wrap break-words text-gray-700 text-body font-normal hover:underline inline-flex w-fit" href="/VibeMarketer_/status/2092243372929151135">1:31 PM · Aug 25, 2026</a><script nonce="Ps0+ToKaq2w4kVj0LZ9ObQ==">!function(options){var element=document.getElementById(options.targetId);if(!element)return;var cached=document.querySelector('[data-timezone]');var timeZone=cached&&cached.dataset.timezone||Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone;element.dataset.timezone=timeZone;element.textContent=(function formatFullTimestamp({ timestamp, lang = "en", timeZone }) {
